Other plant milks like almond or rice milk generally contain far less protein. This makes soy milk a preferred substitute in recipes where protein content matters for texture or nutrition.
  170. The correct answer is D (It is naturally thin and low in protein and fat.): almond milk is mostly water with a small amount of almond solids, giving it a thin body and minimal protein or fat compared to dairy milk or cream. This means sauces made with almond milk will lack the richness and thickening ability of dairy versions unless additional fat or a thickener is used. Almond milk contains no lactose since it is plant based.
  171. The correct answer is A (It produces a stable, creamy microfoam similar to steamed dairy milk.): oat milk's natural starches and beta-glucan fiber help it steam and foam in a way that closely resembles dairy milk, making it popular for lattes and cappuccinos. Soy milk actually has higher protein content, not oat milk. Like other dairy alternatives, oat milk still requires refrigeration and has a limited shelf life once opened.
  172. The correct answer is C (Canned coconut milk has a much higher fat content and richer texture.): canned coconut milk is made from a concentrated extraction of coconut flesh and water, giving it a high fat content that provides body and richness to curries and sauces. Carton coconut milk beverage is diluted and often fortified to mimic dairy milk for drinking. Confusing the two products in a recipe will significantly change the finished dish's consistency.
  173. The correct answer is C (Soy milk and pea protein milk): soy and pea based milks are formulated from legumes naturally rich in protein, giving them a protein content close to dairy milk. Rice, almond, oat, cashew, and hemp milks are generally lower in protein and less capable of providing the structural set needed in custard-style preparations. Choosing the right base is critical when a plant-based dessert relies on protein to set properly.
  174. The correct answer is C (Calcium carbonate, the mineral crystal that forms the rigid porous shell.): the eggshell is composed mostly of calcium carbonate crystals arranged in a porous matrix, which gives it rigidity while still allowing oxygen and carbon dioxide to pass through thousands of tiny pores. This porosity is also why eggs can absorb odors and flavors from their storage environment. The other minerals listed are not significant structural components of the shell.
  175. The correct answer is B (The egg is relatively fresh, since thick albumen breaks down over time.): fresh eggs have a noticeably thick, viscous inner albumen that holds its shape around the yolk when cracked into a pan. As an egg ages, enzymes break down the proteins that create this thickness, causing the white to spread thin and watery. This is one of the visual cues cooks use to judge freshness without checking a date code.
  176. The correct answer is A (They anchor and center the yolk within the surrounding albumen.): the chalazae act like tethers, suspending the yolk in the center of the egg and allowing it to rotate slightly while staying positioned away from the shell. Prominent chalazae are another sign of a fresh egg, since they weaken and become less visible as the egg ages. They contain protein, not fat, and have no role in gas exchange through the shell.
  177. The correct answer is D (Grade A, the top quality tier used in retail and foodservice sale.): Canadian egg grading uses Grade A as the standard for retail and foodservice sale, requiring clean uncracked shells, a small air cell, and firm well-centered yolks with thick albumen. Grade B eggs have minor defects and are typically diverted to processing rather than retail shelves. Grade C does exist in Canada's grading system, but it is reserved for eggs broken out for liquid or powdered egg processing, not sold as shell eggs at retail or used whole in a professional kitchen.
  178. The correct answer is C (Large, the size assumed by default in standard Canadian baking formulas.): Canadian recipes and standardized baking formulas are almost always written assuming large eggs, which fall within a defined minimum weight per dozen. Using peewee, small, or jumbo eggs without adjusting quantities can throw off liquid ratios in baked goods and custards. Professional kitchens should confirm egg size before scaling any recipe that depends on precise egg volume.
  179. The correct answer is B (The internal quality): candling shines a bright light through the shell, allowing an inspector to see the size of the air cell, detect blood spots, and assess yolk position and albumen thickness. It does not measure fat content, bacterial counts, or anything about the hen's diet. This non-destructive method is a key step in assigning the Canadian grade to each egg.
  180. The correct answer is A (Between 0 and 4 degrees Celsius, the correct refrigerated egg storage range.): shell eggs should be kept consistently cold at or below 4 degrees Celsius to slow bacterial growth and preserve interior quality, without freezing them solid. Storing eggs at warmer temperatures accelerates both quality loss and the risk of Salmonella growth. Eggs should also be kept away from strong odors due to the shell's porous surface.
  181. The correct answer is B (A gentle simmer around 82 to 88 degrees Celsius): poaching eggs requires water hot enough to set the proteins gently without violently agitating and tearing the delicate white apart. A full rolling boil breaks the egg into ragged strands, while water that is too cool will not set the white in a reasonable time. A gentle simmer with light bubbles produces a neat, tender poached egg with a runny center.
  182. The correct answer is B (The degree to which the proteins in the yolk and white coagulate.): shorter cooking times set the white while leaving the yolk partially liquid for a soft boiled egg, while longer cooking fully coagulates both the white and yolk proteins for a hard boiled result. Shell color and salt penetration are not meaningfully affected by boiling time. Overcooking can also cause a greenish ring around the yolk from a sulfur reaction.
  183. The correct answer is D (Sunny side up, fried on one side only with a fully liquid yolk.): a sunny side up egg is fried gently on one side only, leaving the yolk fully liquid and the top of the egg unset, resulting in a bright yellow yolk facing upward. Over easy, over medium, and over hard styles all involve flipping the egg to cook the second side to varying degrees of yolk doneness. Basting instead uses spooned fat or a lid to set the top without flipping.
  184. The correct answer is B (Small, soft, creamy curds with a moist finish.): cooking scrambled eggs slowly over gentle heat while stirring constantly allows the proteins to coagulate gradually, forming small, tender curds that stay moist and creamy. High heat cooks the proteins too fast, squeezing out moisture and producing large, dry, rubbery curds. The gentle method is the classic technique taught for high quality scrambled eggs in professional kitchens.
  185. The correct answer is B (Dry heat baking, typically finished under gentle indirect oven heat.): shirred eggs are baked in a buttered ramekin or dish, often with cream or other garnishes, using the oven's ambient heat to set the eggs gently. This differs from poaching, which uses direct contact with simmering liquid, and from frying or sous vide methods. The dry surrounding heat allows even, gentle cooking suited to individual portion service.
  186. The correct answer is B (Lecithin, the phospholipid responsible for binding oil and water together.): lecithin is a phospholipid in egg yolk with both water attracting and fat attracting properties, allowing it to bind oil and water together into a stable emulsion like mayonnaise or hollandaise. Ovalbumin and conalbumin are proteins found mainly in egg white, not associated with emulsification. Avidin is another egg white protein known for binding biotin, unrelated to emulsifying function.
  187. The correct answer is C (The proteins unfold and form a stable network trapping air bubbles.): whipping introduces mechanical energy that unfolds, or denatures, the egg white proteins, which then link together to form a flexible network around incorporated air bubbles. This trapped air expands during baking, giving souffles and meringues their characteristic rise and light texture. The proteins do not dissolve, convert to fat, or crystallize during this process.
  188. The correct answer is A (Egg proteins coagulate with heat, holding other ingredients together.): when heated, egg proteins set and form a firm structure that binds surrounding ingredients like breadcrumbs, ground meat, or vegetables into a cohesive shape. This same coagulation is what makes egg wash adhere breading to a cutlet during frying. The other listed options describe effects eggs do not actually have in a binding application.
  189. The correct answer is D (Temper the yolks by slowly whisking in small amounts of hot dairy first.): tempering gradually raises the temperature of the yolks by whisking in small amounts of the hot liquid before combining everything, preventing the sudden heat shock that would scramble the eggs. Adding cold yolks straight into boiling liquid, using high heat, or skipping stirring all increase the risk of curdling. Gentle, gradual heating and constant stirring keep the sauce smooth.
  190. The correct answer is A (A trace amount of egg yolk fat contaminating the whites during separation.): any fat, including yolk contamination or a greasy bowl, interferes with the proteins' ability to unfold and link together, preventing the whites from building stable volume. A clean, dry stainless bowl, room temperature whites, and a pinch of cream of tartar all actually support better whipping results. Careful separation technique is essential whenever whites are whipped for structure.
  191. The correct answer is B (Swiss or Italian meringue, since both heat the egg whites to a safe temperature.): Swiss meringue is heated over a water bath before whipping and Italian meringue is cooked using hot sugar syrup poured into the whites, both reaching temperatures that reduce bacterial risk. French meringue uses raw whipped whites with no heat treatment, making it unsuitable for uncooked applications like buttercream. Sugar content differences do not address the food safety concern being asked about.
  192. The correct answer is C (Add a thickener or fat, since most plant milks are thinner than cream.): because plant-based milks generally contain far less fat than heavy cream, a cook often needs to compensate with additional roux, a starch slurry, or a fat such as coconut cream to reach a similar body. Simply swapping in plant milk without adjustment usually results in a thin, watery soup. Removing the thickening roux entirely would make the problem worse, not better.
  193. The correct answer is A (Add the acid off the heat and slowly, while the sauce is warm not boiling.): introducing acid gradually into a warm, not boiling, cream sauce and stirring constantly reduces the shock that causes the milk proteins to clump and separate. Bringing the sauce to a rolling boil right after adding acid increases curdling risk significantly. Cream with sufficient fat content is more forgiving than plant-based dairy alternatives, but careful technique is still required.
  194. The correct answer is A (Use pasteurized shell eggs or pasteurized liquid egg product in the dressing.): pasteurized eggs have been heat treated at a controlled temperature to eliminate Salmonella and other pathogens while remaining raw in texture, making them safe for uncooked applications like Caesar dressing. Simply refrigerating longer, rinsing shells, or warming eggs does not eliminate internal bacterial risk. Using pasteurized product is the standard professional solution for any raw egg preparation.
  195. The correct answer is C (Use fresh eggs and create a gentle swirl in barely simmering water.): fresh eggs have thicker albumen that holds together better, and a gentle whirlpool in barely simmering water helps the white wrap around the yolk instead of dispersing. A small amount of vinegar in the water can also help proteins coagulate faster. Old eggs, vigorous boiling, or dropping eggs from a height all increase the chance of ragged, spread out whites.
  196. The correct answer is D (Adding the melted butter too quickly in large amounts all at once.): hollandaise depends on slowly incorporating fat into the yolk mixture so the emulsion can absorb it gradually, and adding butter too fast overwhelms the yolks' emulsifying capacity, causing the sauce to separate or break. Whisking constantly, using gentle heat, and using clarified butter are all correct standard practices that support a stable emulsion rather than causing failure.
  197. The correct answer is D (Folding gently combines the mixtures while preserving trapped air for rise.): folding uses a gentle over and under motion that incorporates the whipped whites into the denser base without knocking out the air bubbles that provide leavening. Vigorous stirring or whisking at this stage would deflate the foam and result in a flat, dense souffle. Preserving that trapped air is the entire purpose of the technique, not speed or convenience.
  198. The correct answer is A (A young, high-moisture cheese melted slowly off direct high heat.): younger cheeses with higher moisture and intact protein structure, like cheddar that is not overly aged, melt more smoothly when incorporated gradually off high direct heat, which prevents the fat from separating out and turning the sauce greasy or grainy. Aged hard cheeses and high heat both increase the risk of a broken, oily sauce. Anti-caking starch on pre-shredded cheese can also interfere with smooth melting in a sauce base.
  199. The correct answer is D (The cream separates into clumped fat solids and a thin, watery liquid.): overwhipping cream continues breaking down the fat globule structure past stiff peaks until the fat clumps together and separates from the liquid buttermilk, essentially beginning the process of churning butter. This is the opposite of becoming smoother or glossier, and the cream does not stay liquid indefinitely. Once separation begins, the cream cannot be restored to a usable whipped texture.
  200. The correct answer is A (Store tightly sealed in the refrigerator at or below 4 degrees Celsius.): opened dairy products should be resealed tightly and kept cold to slow bacterial growth and prevent the cream from absorbing odors from other foods in the refrigerator. Room temperature storage or leaving it uncovered near heat sources accelerates spoilage and creates a food safety hazard. Freezing heavy cream is generally avoided since it can separate and change texture upon thawing, harming quality even if technically extending shelf life.
  201. The correct answer is D (Discard it or use it only in a fully cooked dish reaching a safe temperature.): a cracked shell exposes the interior to potential bacterial contamination from the shell surface, so cracked eggs should either be discarded or used only in dishes that will be thoroughly cooked to a safe internal temperature. Washing the crack does not eliminate the contamination risk and can actually push bacteria through the porous shell. Cracked eggs should never be used in raw or undercooked preparations.
  202. The correct answer is D (Wash hands and sanitize any surfaces or tools that touched the raw egg.): raw shell eggs are a known potential source of Salmonella, so any hands, tools, or surfaces that contact them must be washed and sanitized before touching other food to prevent cross contamination. Continuing to prep other items with contaminated hands or tools risks spreading pathogens to ready to eat foods. Leaving raw egg mixture uncovered at room temperature also creates an unsafe holding condition.
  203. The correct answer is A (Hold the eggs above 60 degrees Celsius and monitor temperature regularly.): cooked egg dishes are potentially hazardous foods that must be held above the minimum hot holding temperature, commonly cited around 60 degrees Celsius in Canadian food safety guidance, with regular temperature checks to confirm they stay out of the danger zone. Room temperature or cool holding allows bacteria to multiply rapidly on a previously cooked product. Uncovered holding without monitoring is not an acceptable food safety practice.
  204. The correct answer is D (Substitute one for one and then adjust seasoning, fat, and liquid as needed.): a reasonable starting point is an equal volume swap, but because plant-based products vary widely in fat, protein, and flavor compared to dairy, the cook must taste and adjust seasoning, added fat, or thickeners to reach a comparable final dish. Never adjusting afterward or automatically doubling liquid ignores the real differences between products. Removing fat entirely rather than substituting defeats the purpose of the conversion.
  205. The correct answer is C (Offer a lactose-free dairy product or a suitable plant-based substitute.): lactose intolerance is a digestive sensitivity to milk sugar, not a protein allergy, so lactose-free dairy products, which have the lactose broken down enzymatically, or a plant-based alternative can both work well. Refusing service outright is unnecessary since safe options exist. Automatically substituting a tree nut product without checking for a nut allergy could introduce a new and more serious safety risk.
  206. The correct answer is C (Cook over low to medium heat, constantly moving the pan and eggs.): a French omelet is cooked gently while the pan is shaken and the eggs are stirred with a fork or spatula, encouraging small curds to form quickly without allowing any part of the surface to brown. High heat, broiling, or added sugar would all promote browning, which is specifically avoided in the classic French style. The result should be a pale, soft, rolled omelet with a delicate texture.
  207. The correct answer is C (A very lightly set, delicate white with a mostly liquid, warm yolk.): coddling uses gentle heat for a short time, just enough to lightly set the white while keeping the yolk warm and mostly liquid, producing a delicate texture distinct from a fully hard boiled egg. It does not involve frying, so there is no browned or crisp exterior. The technique requires careful timing since the gentle heat still continues cooking the egg gradually.
  208. The correct answer is A (It insulates the custard, providing gentle, even heat to prevent curdling.): the surrounding hot water moderates and evens out the oven's heat, keeping the custard's temperature from spiking too high, which would otherwise cause the egg proteins to overcook, curdle, and develop a grainy or weeping texture. It does not speed up cooking or add moisture directly into the mixture, since the ramekins remain sealed from the water. This technique is standard for delicate egg-based baked custards like creme caramel.
  209. The correct answer is B (Whisk in a small amount of cold cream off the heat to re-emulsify.): removing the sauce from heat and whisking in a small amount of cold cream can sometimes help re-form the emulsion by cooling the mixture and reintroducing fresh fat and protein. Continuing to boil or adding more acid will worsen the separation rather than fix it. Straining out the liquid abandons the sauce rather than attempting to correct the technique error.
  210. The correct answer is B (It acts as an adhesive layer, helping the breadcrumbs stick securely.): the egg wash, typically beaten egg with a small amount of water or milk, provides a sticky, protein-rich layer that the dry breadcrumbs can adhere to after the flour coats the moist surface of the cutlet. It does not cook the cutlet or function as a seasoning step, and flour is still needed beforehand to help the egg wash itself adhere evenly. Skipping the egg wash typically results in breading that falls off during frying.
  211. The correct answer is A (The milk solids and water are removed, leaving pure butterfat.): clarifying butter involves gently melting it and skimming off the milk solids and water, leaving behind pure butterfat that has a significantly higher smoke point than whole butter. It is the milk solids that burn and blacken at high heat, so removing them is the entire purpose of the technique. The fat itself is retained, not removed, and salt removal is not the goal of clarification.
  212. The correct answer is B (The live cultures in the buttermilk ferment and thicken the cream mixture.): the active bacterial cultures present in buttermilk begin fermenting the lactose in the cream at room temperature, gradually thickening the mixture and developing the characteristic tang associated with creme fraiche. The cream is not simply chilling, since it is left at room temperature specifically to encourage fermentation. This process does not pasteurize the mixture and is unrelated to physically separating fat from liquid.
  213. The correct answer is B (Remove the base from heat and stir in cheese gradually off direct heat.): removing the sauce from direct high heat and stirring in cheese gradually allows it to melt smoothly into the base without the proteins overheating and separating from the fat, which is what causes a greasy, stringy texture. Adding cheese all at once to boiling liquid or raising the heat further would make the separation worse. The roux base is still needed to provide the starch structure the sauce depends on.
  214. The correct answer is B (Plant-based cheese alternatives lack the casein protein structure of dairy cheese.): the characteristic browning and stretch of melted dairy mozzarella comes from casein proteins and fat interacting under heat, a structure that plant-based alternatives built from starches and oils generally cannot fully replicate. This is a product formulation issue, not an oven temperature problem, and plant-based cheeses are not inherently higher in protein than dairy cheese. Some formulations are specifically engineered to improve melt and stretch, but differences typically remain.
  215. The correct answer is A (Add a small amount of gelatin, mascarpone, or powdered sugar as a stabilizer.): incorporating a stabilizer such as bloomed gelatin, mascarpone cheese, or powdered sugar with its added cornstarch helps whipped cream hold its structure and resist weeping over extended holding times. Whipping with no additions and holding at room temperature would cause the cream to deflate and separate faster. Adding more liquid or underwhipping would both weaken rather than strengthen the foam's stability.
  216. The correct answer is C (Choosing an unsweetened, unflavored plant milk with good heat stability.): a savory application requires a plant milk without added sweeteners or flavorings like vanilla, and one that has been tested or known to hold up under sustained simmering without separating. Cost alone should not drive the selection when consistency and flavor compatibility matter to the finished dish. Products marketed for cold cereal use may not have the heat stability needed for a simmered soup.
  217. The correct answer is A (The butter was added too quickly or the mixture overheated during whisking.): a broken hollandaise is almost always caused by adding fat faster than the yolks can absorb it or by overheating the yolks until their emulsifying proteins can no longer hold the mixture together. Egg size, a properly balanced amount of lemon juice, and whisk material are not typical root causes of this specific failure. Diagnosing the true cause allows the cook to correct pace and temperature control on the next attempt.
  218. The correct answer is B (The dish must be held above the minimum hot holding temperature the entire time.): egg dishes are potentially hazardous and must remain continuously above the required hot holding temperature throughout the entire four-hour service, not just checked once at the start, since temperatures can drift over an extended buffet period. Room temperature holding or a single initial check would allow the dish to fall into the temperature danger zone unnoticed. Serving cold would require an entirely different preparation and holding approach, not a simple reclassification of the same hot dish.
  219. The correct answer is A (The rapid temperature change caused the trapped air structure to contract.): souffles rise because trapped air and steam expand at high oven heat, but once removed from the oven the structure cools quickly and the air contracts, causing an inevitable amount of deflation that is a normal characteristic of this dish, not a preparation error. Underfilling the dish or cheese aging time are unrelated to this immediate post-bake collapse. Opening the oven door mid-bake could cause premature collapse during baking, but this scenario describes collapse after removal, which is expected physics.
  220. The correct answer is B (Match each dish to a plant milk suited to its fat, flavor, and heat needs.): the panna cotta likely benefits from a richer, higher fat option like a full-fat coconut or cashew milk for body and mouthfeel, while the savory soup needs an unsweetened, heat-stable, neutral flavored milk that will not curdle or taste sweet. Using the same product for both ignores their very different functional and flavor requirements. Substituting plain water would remove the fat and body both dishes depend on entirely.
  221. The correct answer is B (The eggs were held too long at excessive heat, causing continued protein tightening and moisture release.): prolonged hot holding continues to cook already-set egg proteins, tightening them further and squeezing out moisture, which produces both a rubbery texture and a pool of watery liquid in the pan. Undercooking would not explain rubbery texture, since undercooked eggs are typically soft rather than tough. This is a common banquet-service problem addressed by using techniques and additions designed to hold up better under extended heat.
  222. The correct answer is C (A dairy allergy can trigger a severe immune reaction, requiring strict avoidance of dairy protein.): a true dairy allergy involves the immune system reacting to milk proteins and can cause a severe or even life-threatening reaction, requiring strict prevention of any dairy protein and cross-contact in the kitchen. Lactose intolerance is a digestive sensitivity to milk sugar that causes discomfort but is not immune-mediated or life-threatening. Treating both conditions identically risks either unnecessary restriction or, more dangerously, insufficient precaution for the allergic guest.
  223. The correct answer is A (One tablespoon of ground flaxseed mixed with three tablespoons of water.): the standard flax egg ratio combines one tablespoon of ground flaxseed with three tablespoons of water and lets it sit until it thickens into a gel that mimics an egg's binding property in baked goods. Using a whole unmixed flaxseed or flaxseed oil would not replicate this binding function, and a full cup of ground flax to a single tablespoon of water is far too concentrated for a one egg substitution. Correct ratios matter for achieving the intended texture in the final product.
  224. The correct answer is D (The custard was baked at too high a temperature or for too long.): egg proteins in a custard overcoagulate when exposed to excessive heat or time, squeezing out moisture and creating a curdled, grainy texture instead of a smooth set. Straining the mixture and using a water bath are both correct techniques that help produce a smoother custard, not causes of the problem. A difference between large and extra large eggs is a minor variable unlikely to be the primary cause of significant curdling.
  225. The correct answer is D (Use the fresh eggs for poaching and the older eggs for hard boiling and peeling.): fresh eggs have thick albumen that holds together well during poaching, producing a neater result, while slightly older eggs have a higher internal pH and a shell membrane that releases more easily, making them noticeably easier to peel after hard boiling. Reversing the allocation would make poaching messier and peeling more difficult. Discarding a perfectly usable batch of eggs would be wasteful when both can be matched to an appropriate task.
  226. The correct answer is D (Half and half does not contain enough milk fat to trap air stably.): half and half typically contains around 10 to 18 percent fat, well below the roughly 30 to 35 percent minimum fat needed to form a stable network of fat globules capable of holding whipped air bubbles. No amount of additional whipping time will compensate for the insufficient fat content, and heating would not resolve the underlying structural limitation. There is no enzyme in half and half that specifically blocks whipping.
  227. The correct answer is A (A reaction between yolk iron and white sulfur from overcooking, prevented by cooling eggs promptly.): overcooking releases hydrogen sulfide from the egg white, which reacts with iron in the yolk to form a gray-green ferrous sulfide layer at the yolk's surface. Promptly cooling eggs in an ice water bath after cooking slows this reaction and helps prevent the ring from forming. This discoloration is a preventable technique issue, not an inherent pigment or a contamination problem from the cooking pot.
  228. The correct answer is C (Portion it into smaller, shallower containers and use an ice bath or blast chiller.): dividing a large hot item into shallow portions increases surface area for faster heat loss, and using an ice bath or blast chiller helps move the food through the temperature danger zone quickly to meet food safety cooling guidelines. Leaving it at room temperature or placing a large hot mass directly into a refrigerator or freezer can raise the ambient temperature of stored food nearby and cools far too slowly at the center. Rapid, controlled cooling is required for potentially hazardous foods like an egg and cream based quiche.
  229. The correct answer is C (A high-fat, unsweetened product like full-fat coconut cream with heat stability.): higher fat content in a plant-based product, such as full-fat coconut cream, provides more resistance to breaking or scorching under high, direct heat compared to thin, low-fat plant milks. A sweetened cold-drinking beverage would introduce unwanted sugar into a savory finishing sauce and likely lacks the fat needed for stability. Fat content has a significant, measurable effect on how a dairy alternative performs under high heat searing conditions.
  230. The correct answer is B (Undercooking the meringue or excess humidity preventing the proteins from fully stabilizing.): weeping occurs when the egg white proteins in the meringue are not sufficiently cooked or stabilized, often due to undercooking, excess ambient humidity, or undissolved sugar, allowing liquid to seep out as the structure sits. Fully dissolving sugar before baking is actually a preventive step, not a cause. Spreading meringue over a completely cooled filling, rather than a hot one, is a separate technique issue that can contribute to shrinkage but is not the primary driver of weeping described here.
  231. The correct answer is D (Durum wheat semolina, a coarse high protein flour): Semolina has a high protein content and coarse texture that gives dried pasta its firm bite. It also allows the pasta to hold a long shelf life at room temperature without spoiling. Fresh pasta, by contrast, typically uses soft wheat or all purpose flour with eggs.
  232. The correct answer is D (Whole egg mixed directly into the base flour): Fresh Italian style pasta such as tagliatelle and fettuccine is typically built with whole eggs mixed into the flour. Most dried factory shapes like penne and spaghetti are traditionally eggless, water based doughs. Assuming all pasta contains egg is a common exam trap.
  233. The correct answer is C (About one hundred grams of flour per whole egg): Fresh pasta dough is typically built on a ratio of one whole egg per 100 grams of flour. Kitchens often adjust this ratio with extra yolks for a richer, more tender texture. This ratio serves as a reliable starting point before adjusting for hydration.
  234. The correct answer is D (A gluten network that gives the dough elasticity): Kneading develops gluten, the protein network that gives pasta dough its elasticity. This elasticity lets the dough stretch thin without tearing when rolled. Resting the dough afterward relaxes the gluten so it rolls out evenly.
  235. The correct answer is D (It prevents a dry skin from forming on the surface): Wrapping the dough tightly in plastic keeps air away from the surface. This prevents a dry, hardened skin from forming while the dough rests. A dry skin can create tough spots and cracking when the dough is rolled.
  236. The correct answer is C (Semolina and water dough, eggless and rustic in texture): This dough family skips egg entirely, relying on semolina and water for structure. It produces a chewier, more rustic texture suited to hand formed shapes like orecchiette and cavatelli. Plain egg dough is instead the base for ribbon pastas like tagliatelle.
  237. The correct answer is D (Moisture increases, so less egg or liquid is needed): Squid ink adds moisture to the dough, changing its hydration balance. To keep the dough workable, slightly less egg or liquid is used than in a standard recipe. Flavoured doughs in general require this kind of hydration adjustment.
  238. The correct answer is D (Sudden compression can tear the dough or cause uneven thickness): Jumping straight to the thinnest setting compresses the dough too fast. This can tear the sheet or leave it uneven in thickness. Gradual progression through each setting keeps the sheet consistent and intact.
  239. The correct answer is C (Extruded pasta forced through a specifically shaped die): Extruded pasta uses a machine that forces dough through a shaped die. This method creates tubes such as bucatini and ridged shapes such as rigatoni. Rolled pasta instead flattens dough into sheets using a machine or rolling pin.
  240. The correct answer is D (A dumpling made from riced potato, not a true wheat pasta): Potato gnocchi uses riced cooked potato bound with flour and egg rather than a wheat dough. This makes it a dumpling rather than a true pasta despite its common grouping with pasta dishes. Overworking the dough develops too much gluten and makes it dense.
  241. The correct answer is D (Kansui, an alkaline mineral water mixed into the flour): Ramen noodles are made with wheat flour and kansui, an alkaline mineral water. Kansui gives the noodle its yellow colour and springy, firm bite. Udon noodles are made without kansui, giving them a softer, milder character.
  242. The correct answer is B (Rice flour combined simply with plain drinking water.): Rice based noodles such as vermicelli, pad thai noodles, and rice sticks are made from rice flour and water. They are gluten free, unlike wheat based noodles such as ramen or udon. These noodles are often soaked in warm water rather than boiled from dry.
  243. The correct answer is A (They are glass noodles or cellophane noodles, turning translucent.): Glass noodles, or cellophane noodles, are made from mung bean starch. They turn translucent when cooked and have a springy, slightly chewy texture. This distinguishes them from opaque rice or wheat based noodles.
  244. The correct answer is C (Buckwheat flour, sometimes blended with plain wheat flour): Soba noodles are made from buckwheat flour, sometimes blended with wheat flour. This gives them a nutty flavour and a greyish brown colour. Soba is traditionally served cold with a dipping sauce or hot in broth.
  245. The correct answer is B (Boiling can make the delicate noodles mushy or cause breakage): Rice noodles are delicate and prolonged boiling breaks down their starch structure. Soaking them gently in warm water avoids this mushy, broken result. They are often finished with a brief stir fry or blanch afterward.
  246. The correct answer is B (Ravioli, formed by sealing filling between two sheets of dough): Ravioli is made by placing small mounds of filling on one sheet, covering with a second sheet, and cutting into squares or rounds. Removing trapped air pockets around each mound is critical before cutting. Tortellini instead uses a folded and wrapped ring shape.
  247. The correct answer is B (Trapped air can cause the sealed pasta to burst during cooking): Air trapped inside a ravioli expands when heated in boiling water. This expansion can cause the sealed edges to burst open during cooking. Pressing out air pockets before sealing prevents this production fault.
  248. The correct answer is D (The two outer points are wrapped around a finger and pinched): After folding into a triangle or half moon, the two outer points are wrapped around a finger. Pinching them together forms the characteristic ring shape of tortellini. Tortelloni is the larger version, typically filled with vegetable or cheese mixtures.
  249. The correct answer is D (A poor seal that allows filling to leak and water to enter): A poor seal is the single most common production fault in stuffed pasta. Gaps in the seal allow filling to leak out and water to enter during boiling. This results in a soggy or burst final product.
  250. The correct answer is C (Plain flour, one whole egg, and plain water): Perogies are made from an unleavened dough of flour, egg, and water. Common fillings include mashed potato and cheddar, sauerkraut, or seasoned meat. They are usually boiled and often finished by pan frying in butter with onions.

Practice Test 3 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (Pan fry and steam, ending with the lid removed to crisp): The classic gyoza method sears the dumplings first, then adds water and covers the pan to steam them through. The lid is removed at the end to let the water evaporate and crisp the bottom again. This technique produces a crisp base with a tender top.
  2. The correct answer is C (Wonton wrappers are thinner and more delicate, so folding is simpler): Wonton wrappers are thinner and more delicate than gyoza wrappers. Because of this delicacy, wontons are typically folded into a simple triangle or purse shape. Gyoza wrappers are sturdier and often pleated instead.
  3. The correct answer is A (It means firm to the tooth, describing slight resistance when bitten.): Al dente translates to firm to the tooth. It describes pasta with a slight resistance when bitten, with no raw centre and no mushy exterior. The only reliable test for this doneness is tasting a piece directly.
  4. The correct answer is B (It seasons the pasta from within since seasoning it after is ineffective): Salting the cooking water seasons the pasta as it absorbs liquid during cooking. Pasta cannot be seasoned effectively once it has finished cooking. A large volume of well salted, rapidly boiling water is the standard method.
  5. The correct answer is C (Fresh pasta has not gone through a drying and hardening process): Fresh pasta has not gone through a drying and hardening process like dried pasta has. Thin fresh pasta such as tagliolini may need only one to two minutes to cook. Dried pasta shapes commonly need eight to twelve minutes depending on thickness.
  6. The correct answer is B (It helps sauces emulsify and cling more evenly to the pasta): The starch in reserved pasta water helps sauces emulsify and bind to the pasta. A splash can also loosen a sauce that has become too thick during the final toss. This is a standard professional habit in pasta cookery.
  7. The correct answer is D (Long thin strands such as spaghetti and angel hair): Thin strands pair best with light, smooth sauces since a thick sauce cannot cling evenly to them. Wide ribbon pasta instead pairs with richer, heavier sauces due to greater surface area. Tubular shapes are designed to trap chunky sauces inside their hollow centres.
  8. The correct answer is D (It should be tossed with a little oil and cooled quickly): Cooked pasta held for later service should be tossed lightly with oil to prevent sticking. It should also be cooled quickly if not served immediately and reheated properly before service. This avoids holding it at unsafe temperatures for extended periods.
  9. The correct answer is D (Add a few drops of water or oil worked in gradually): A dough that feels too dry can be corrected with a few drops of water or oil. This is worked in gradually to avoid overcorrecting the hydration. A dough that is too wet is instead corrected with a light dusting of flour.
  10. The correct answer is B (Add extra egg yolks while reducing the number of whole eggs): Yolks add fat and emulsifying power without adding as much water as whole eggs. A dough with more yolks and fewer whole eggs becomes firmer, more golden, and easier to roll thin. This is a common professional adjustment to the base ratio.
  11. The correct answer is D (The dough is likely to tear or end up unevenly thick): Sudden compression from skipping thickness settings can tear the dough. It can also produce a sheet with uneven thickness across its surface. Rolling should progress gradually through decreasing thickness settings instead.
  12. The correct answer is C (Hand formed pasta, shaped entirely by the cook's hands): Hand formed pasta relies entirely on the cook's hands and simple tools. Orecchiette is thumbed into a small ear shape as a classic example of this category. Cavatelli is shaped similarly by rolling and pressing with fingers or a ridged board.
  13. The correct answer is B (The gnocchi will develop too much gluten and become dense and rubbery): Overworking gnocchi dough develops too much gluten, since flour is present even though potato is the main ingredient. This excess gluten produces a dense, rubbery final result. The dough should be handled as little and as gently as possible.
  14. The correct answer is C (The noodles turn mushy and are likely to break apart): Rice noodles are delicate and prolonged boiling breaks down their starch structure. This produces a mushy result and can cause the noodles to break apart. Rice noodles should instead be soaked in warm water and finished by stir frying or brief blanching.
  15. The correct answer is C (These noodles are more commonly called glass or cellophane noodles): Mung bean starch noodles are commonly called glass noodles or cellophane noodles. They turn translucent when cooked and have a springy, slightly chewy texture, making them suitable for cold salads. This distinguishes them from wheat based ramen or udon.
  16. The correct answer is B (Press out the air pockets around each mound before sealing the pasta): Trapped air pockets must be pressed out around each filling mound before the pasta is sealed and cut. Leaving air inside can cause the ravioli to burst during cooking. This step is critical to producing an intact final product.
  17. The correct answer is B (Excess moisture can soak through the dough or weaken the seal): A filling that is too wet can soak through the dough during storage. It can also cause the seal to fail during cooking, leading to leaks. Ricotta based fillings should always be well drained before use.
  18. The correct answer is C (Fully cook and cool the meat before it is piped into the pasta): Meat based fillings should be fully cooked and cooled before being piped or spooned onto the dough. Raw meat inside sealed pasta creates a food safety risk if cooking time is insufficient. This step protects against undercooked filling reaching the guest.
  19. The correct answer is C (A light egg wash or water brushed along the dough edges): Sealing is done with a light egg wash or water brushed along the edges of the dough. This acts as glue holding the two sheets together securely. Dry flour alone does not create a reliable bond between the layers.
  20. The correct answer is D (Additional flavour and browning added after the boiling step): Perogies are boiled first to fully cook the dough and filling. Pan frying in butter with onions afterward adds browning, flavour, and textural contrast. This two step process is the traditional finishing method for perogies.
  21. The correct answer is C (The lid is removed to let the bottom crisp again): Once the water has evaporated during the pan fry and steam method, the lid is removed. This allows the bottom of the gyoza to crisp again after steaming. This final step restores the textural contrast the technique is known for.
  22. The correct answer is B (The thin, delicate wonton wrapper is not suited to complex pleating): Wonton wrappers are thinner and more delicate than gyoza wrappers. They are typically folded into a simple triangle or purse shape rather than pleated. Applying gyoza style pleating to a delicate wonton wrapper risks tearing it.
  23. The correct answer is B (The thicker perogy dough remains undercooked in the centre): Perogy dough is thicker than a delicate wonton wrapper and needs a longer boil to cook through. Assuming thicker wrappers cook the same as thin wrappers is a common exam trap. The result of a short boil would be an undercooked, doughy centre.
  24. The correct answer is D (Package times are only a guideline and can vary by batch and altitude): Cook times on packaging are only a starting guideline. They can vary with altitude, water volume, and the specific batch of pasta. The only reliable way to test doneness is to remove a piece and taste it.
  25. The correct answer is A (The pasta should be drained a minute or two before full doneness): The pasta is drained a minute or two before it reaches full doneness for this finishing technique. It is then added directly to a hot sauce along with a splash of starchy pasta water. The two are tossed together until the pasta finishes cooking in the sauce.
  26. The correct answer is B (A shape meant for heavier sauces is paired with one too thin to cling): Wide ribbon pasta such as fettuccine pairs well with rich, creamy, or heavier sauces due to its broad surface area. A thin, brothy sauce does not cling as effectively to this shape. Matching sauce weight to pasta shape is a foundational tested skill.
  27. The correct answer is A (Brown butter and sage, so the delicate filling is not overpowered): Delicate stuffed pasta such as ravioli is usually served with simple sauces like brown butter and sage. This prevents the sauce from overpowering the filling or masking the work in the pasta. A heavy ragu would be more appropriate for a sturdier tubular shape.
  28. The correct answer is D (Refrigerate it tightly wrapped and use it within a day or two): Fresh pasta dough should be stored under refrigeration, tightly wrapped or lightly floured on a tray. It should be used within a day or two for best quality and food safety. Freezing is an option for longer storage but requires freezing in a single layer first.
  29. The correct answer is A (Freeze the pieces in a single layer on a tray before bagging): Fresh pasta should be frozen in a single layer on a tray before being bagged. This prevents individual pieces from clumping into a solid mass. Once frozen solid, the pieces can then be transferred to a bag for storage.
  30. The correct answer is A (Hold either hot above sixty degrees or cold below four degrees Celsius): Stuffed items with raw meat or seafood fillings carry a higher food safety risk. They must be held either hot above 60 degrees Celsius or cold below 4 degrees Celsius. This follows standard time and temperature control for safety guidelines.
  31. The correct answer is A (Reheat the pasta quickly in boiling water or sauce for a short time): At service time, par cooked pasta is reheated quickly in boiling water or sauce for a short period to finish cooking. This lets the kitchen serve pasta quickly during a rush while achieving proper final texture. The par cooked product must also be documented and dated correctly.
  32. The correct answer is B (Yes, thick udon noodles can carry a heartier, more concentrated sauce): Thick udon noodles can carry a heartier, more concentrated sauce or broth. This follows the same shape to sauce logic used in Italian pasta pairing. Thin rice vermicelli instead works better with light broths or thin dressings.
  33. The correct answer is A (Place it in a sealed container in a cool, dry area): Dried commercial pasta is shelf stable and should be stored in a cool, dry area away from moisture. Once the original packaging is opened, it should go into sealed containers. This prevents pest contamination and preserves quality.
  34. The correct answer is A (The base flour or liquid amount may need adjusting to stay workable): Enriched or flavoured doughs add purees or powders that change colour, flavour, and hydration. The base flour or liquid must be adjusted to keep the dough workable after these additions. This is a general principle across all flavoured pasta doughs.
  35. The correct answer is A (It is designed to trap chunky sauce inside its centre and ridges): Tubular and ridged shapes such as penne are designed to trap chunky sauces inside their hollow centres or along ridged surfaces. This makes them ideal for meat ragus or vegetable heavy sauces. Smooth thin strands would not hold this type of sauce as effectively.
  36. The correct answer is C (A vegetable or cheese based filling mixture typically.): Tortelloni is the larger cousin of tortellini, typically filled with a vegetable or cheese mixture rather than meat. It is folded in a similar wrapped style but at a bigger scale. This distinguishes it from the often meat filled standard tortellini.
  37. The correct answer is D (Gnocchi is a potato dumpling, and overworking causes a dense result, not tearing.): Gnocchi's structure comes from riced potato rather than a kneaded wheat dough. Because of this, its handling risk is overworking into a dense, rubbery result, not tearing from underdeveloped gluten. Treating it identically to egg pasta ignores this fundamental difference in composition.
  38. The correct answer is C (Excess moisture will weaken the seal, raising the risk of bursting): A filling that is too wet can soak through the dough during storage and compromise the seal before cooking even begins. Once boiled, this weakened seal is far more likely to burst or produce a soggy result. Draining ricotta based fillings is a preventative step, not a cosmetic one.
  39. The correct answer is B (Failing to account for the large gap in cook time between fresh and dried pasta): Fresh pasta such as tagliolini may need only one to two minutes, while dried pasta commonly needs eight to twelve minutes. Cooking both together with one shared time will overcook the fresh pasta or undercook the dried pasta. Each type must be timed and tested independently.
  40. The correct answer is A (Matching sauce weight to noodle thickness, since a heavy sauce overwhelms thin noodles): Thin rice vermicelli works well in light broths or thin dressings, not heavy concentrated sauces. Overwhelming a delicate noodle with a thick, heavy sauce is a recognized production mistake. Sauce weight should always be matched to noodle thickness and character.
  41. The correct answer is B (Freezing does not cook meat, so the pasta must fully cook it later during boiling): Freezing preserves food but does not cook it or reliably eliminate all pathogens. Meat fillings should be fully cooked and cooled before being sealed into pasta in the first place. Relying on a later boil to fully cook raw meat inside sealed dough is an unreliable food safety practice.
  42. The correct answer is A (Squid ink adds moisture, so the standard ratio produces an overly wet dough): Squid ink dough adds moisture, so slightly less egg or liquid is typically needed to reach the right consistency. Using the standard unmodified ratio results in an overly wet, sticky dough. Flavoured doughs generally require this kind of hydration adjustment to remain workable.
  43. The correct answer is A (Wide pasta suits heavier sauces, so a thin watery one fails to cling): Wide ribbon pasta such as pappardelle pairs well with rich, creamy, or heavier sauces because of its broad surface area. A thin, watery sauce lacks the body to cling to this surface and instead pools in the bowl. This illustrates why shape to sauce matching is a core production skill, not just tradition.
  44. A countertop temperature around 30 degrees Celsius falls squarely in the unsafe temperature danger zone. This violates standard time and temperature control for safety guidelines regardless of an earlier pre cook step.
  45. The correct answer is B (Perogy dough is thicker and needs a longer boil to cook fully through): Perogies use a thicker, more substantial dough than the thin wonton wrapper. A shared boiling time built around the thinner wrapper would leave perogy dough undercooked in the centre. Treating thicker wrappers the same as thin wrappers is a recognized exam trap for this reason.
  46. The correct answer is A (Residual heat keeps cooking the pasta, which may overcook or clump): The cold water shock step in par cooking stops the cooking process quickly. Skipping it allows residual heat to keep cooking the pasta, risking overcooking or clumping together. This undermines the entire purpose of the par cook method, which is to preserve a precise final texture at reheat.
  47. The correct answer is A (Udon skips kansui, giving it a softer character than springy ramen): Ramen noodles use kansui, an alkaline mineral water, for their yellow colour and springy chew. Udon noodles are made without kansui, resulting in a softer, milder noodle overall. Being wheat based does not mean two noodle types share the same processing method or resulting texture.
  48. The correct answer is A (Finishing the pasta directly in hot sauce with starchy pasta water added): Finishing pasta in the pan involves draining it slightly underdone and tossing it with hot sauce and starchy pasta water. This produces a better bound, glossier final dish than simply pouring sauce over fully cooked pasta. Reserved pasta water is key to helping the sauce cling properly.
  49. The correct answer is D (Many traditional dried shapes like penne and spaghetti are eggless already.): Egg is the defining ingredient of fresh Italian style pasta, not of the category as a whole. Many traditional dried shapes like penne, fusilli, and spaghetti are eggless, water based doughs. Assuming all pasta needs egg is a well known and testable misconception.
  50. The correct answer is B (The seal along the dough edges failed, likely from insufficient egg wash): A poor seal is the single most common production fault in stuffed pasta. Gaps in the seal allow filling to leak out and water to enter during boiling. This produces exactly the cloudy pot and soggy dough symptoms described in the scenario.
  51. The correct answer is A (One part rice to two parts liquid, simmered covered then rested): Long grain white rice is cooked with a 1 to 2 ratio of rice to liquid. It is simmered covered for about 18 to 20 minutes then rested off heat before fluffing. This ratio produces separate, fluffy grains typical of long grain varieties.
  52. The correct answer is D (Brown rice keeps its bran layer intact, which slows water absorption considerably.): The intact bran layer on brown rice slows the penetration of water into the starchy endosperm. This means brown rice needs a higher liquid ratio and a longer simmer time than white rice. Substituting one for the other without adjusting time and liquid produces poor results.
  53. The correct answer is A (Rice is sauteed in fat with aromatics, then simmered covered and undisturbed.): The pilaf method starts with sauteing rice in fat with aromatics until coated, then adding hot liquid and cooking covered without stirring. Stirring breaks the grains and releases excess starch. This fat coating step is what defines pilaf and separates it from simple boiling.
  54. The correct answer is C (Arborio, carnaroli, or vialone nano rice, all high in starch.): These varieties are high in amylopectin starch, which releases during constant stirring to create risotto's creamy texture. Hot stock is added gradually while stirring throughout cooking. This differs completely from the pilaf method of covered, undisturbed cooking.
  55. The correct answer is A (All onda, meaning the risotto spreads slightly and flows across the plate): Properly finished risotto should have a loose, flowing consistency described as all onda in the trade. It should spread slightly when plated rather than holding a stiff mound. This texture comes from the starch released during constant stirring.
  56. The correct answer is D (Rinsing removes bitter tasting saponins naturally present on the seed coat.): Quinoa naturally carries saponins on its seed coat, a compound that tastes bitter and soapy if left on. Rinsing thoroughly before cooking removes this coating. Quinoa is also considered a complete protein containing all nine essential amino acids.
  57. The correct answer is A (The germ ring separates and appears as a small curl on each grain.): When quinoa finishes cooking, the germ ring separates from the seed and becomes visible as a small curl. This is the standard doneness indicator used in professional kitchens. Quinoa is generally simmered about 15 minutes using a 1 to 2 ratio of quinoa to liquid.
  58. The correct answer is C (Pearl barley has had the bran polished off and therefore cooks faster.): Pearl barley has been polished to remove the bran, giving it a softer texture and a shorter cooking time of about 25 to 30 minutes. Pot barley retains more bran and takes 45 to 60 minutes to cook. Because the bran and germ are removed, pearl barley is not considered a whole grain.
  59. The correct answer is D (Young green wheat that has been roasted and then cracked before packaging.): Freekeh is harvested while the wheat is still young and green, then roasted and cracked. This process gives it a distinct smoky flavor and chewy bite. It is treated as a whole grain in professional kitchens.
  60. The correct answer is B (The wheat kernels are parboiled, dried, and then cracked before packaging): Bulgur wheat goes through a parboiling, drying, and cracking process. Because of this pre-cooking, bulgur can often be rehydrated with hot liquid alone rather than requiring active simmering. This makes it useful for quick tabbouleh-style preparations.
  61. The correct answer is A (Steel cut oats are simply chopped whole groats, which cook more slowly.): Steel cut oats are simply chopped whole oat groats, so they retain more texture and require a longer cooking time. Rolled oats are steamed and flattened, which shortens their cooking time considerably. Both come from the same oat groat but are processed differently.
  62. The correct answer is C (Lentils, which have thin skins and cook relatively quickly without soaking.): Lentils have thin skins and cook relatively quickly, typically in 20 to 30 minutes, so they do not require soaking before cooking. Most other dried beans and chickpeas benefit from a long or quick soak first. This is a commonly tested exception among pulses.
  63. The correct answer is D (Eight to twelve hours refrigerated, or left to soak overnight instead.): A long soak covers beans in cold water and refrigerates them for 8 to 12 hours or overnight. This rehydrates the beans and shortens the eventual cooking time. A quick soak alternative involves a brief boil followed by an hour of resting off heat.
  64. The correct answer is B (Red or yellow split lentils, which break down into a soft puree): Red and yellow split lentils cook the fastest among lentil types and break down into a soft puree. This makes them well suited for dal and for thickening soups. Green, brown, and French lentils hold their shape much better during cooking.
  65. The correct answer is A (Early salt can toughen bean skins and slow how quickly water is absorbed.): Adding salt too early can toughen bean skins and slow how quickly they absorb water. Many kitchens wait until partway through cooking or near the end to add salt. Acidic ingredients have an even stronger softening-delay effect and should also be added later.
  66. The correct answer is C (A naturally occurring toxin in the beans called phytohaemagglutinin.): Dried kidney beans naturally contain phytohaemagglutinin, which must be destroyed before the beans are safe to eat. This requires boiling at a rolling boil for at least 10 minutes before finishing at a simmer. Slow cookers that never reach a full boil can actually increase toxin levels if beans are not pre-boiled.
  67. The correct answer is C (A rolling boil maintained for a minimum of ten full minutes.): Dried kidney beans must be boiled hard for at least 10 minutes to destroy the natural toxin phytohaemagglutinin before finishing cooking at a lower simmer. Skipping this step, or relying only on a low simmer, leaves the toxin intact. This safety step is a frequently tested exam point.
  68. The correct answer is D (Calcium and magnesium ions interfere with pectin breakdown in the bean skins.): Minerals in hard water, particularly calcium and magnesium, interfere with the pectin breakdown needed for beans to soften. A small amount of baking soda can help counteract this effect. Too much baking soda, however, damages flavor and texture.
  69. The correct answer is C (The Maillard reaction combined with caramelization of natural sugars present): Toasting develops flavor through the Maillard reaction between proteins and sugars, along with caramelization of natural sugars present in the nut or seed. This also crisps the texture. High fat content means these items can burn quickly, so frequent stirring is required.
  70. The correct answer is D (Residual heat continues cooking the almonds, which can lead to overcooking): Nuts continue cooking briefly from residual heat even after being removed from a pan or oven. Spreading them on a cool tray stops this carryover cooking. Pulling them just before they look fully toasted prevents burning.
  71. The correct answer is A (Nuts and seeds contain high levels of unsaturated fats that oxidize readily): Nuts and seeds are high in unsaturated fats, which oxidize readily when exposed to heat, light, and air, leading to rancidity. Shelled nuts should be kept in airtight containers in a cool, dark place. Refrigeration or freezing extends shelf life further.
  72. The correct answer is B (Sesame seeds, used for garnish and pressed into tahini): Sesame is one of Canada's recognized priority food allergens alongside tree nuts and peanuts. Cooks must prevent cross contact through dedicated equipment or thorough cleaning and sanitizing. Clear labeling and communication with servers and guests is a professional responsibility.
  73. The correct answer is C (The transfer of an allergen between foods through shared equipment or surfaces): Cross contact happens when an allergen moves from one food to another via shared tools, cutting boards, or cooking surfaces. It is just as dangerous as the allergen being an intended ingredient. Dedicated equipment or thorough sanitizing between uses prevents this hazard.
  74. The correct answer is B (Peanuts can trigger allergic reactions that are similar to tree nut reactions.): Peanuts are technically a legume, not a tree nut, but they are grouped with tree nuts on allergen labels because they can trigger similarly severe reactions. This grouping helps protect guests with allergies. Cooks should still treat peanuts as a distinct priority allergen alongside tree nuts.
  75. The correct answer is A (Coagulated soy milk that has been pressed together into solid blocks): Tofu is produced by coagulating soy milk and pressing the resulting curds into blocks. This process is conceptually similar to how dairy cheese is made from coagulated milk. Tofu is available in a range of firmness levels depending on how much moisture is pressed out.
  76. The correct answer is B (Extra firm tofu, which has the lowest moisture content of all types): Extra firm tofu has the lowest moisture content of the tofu types, which allows it to hold its shape well under high heat. This makes it the right choice for grilling, pan-searing, deep frying, or stir-frying. Silken tofu, by contrast, is best reserved for blending into sauces and desserts.
  77. The correct answer is A (Whole soybeans that are cooked and fermented with a mold culture.): Tempeh is made by cooking whole soybeans, then fermenting them with a mold culture that binds them into a firm, dense cake. This differs from tofu, which uses pressed soy milk rather than whole beans. Tempeh has a firmer texture and a nuttier, earthier flavor as a result.
  78. The correct answer is D (Vital wheat gluten remaining after starch is washed out of dough): Seitan is produced from vital wheat gluten, which is the protein left behind after the starch has been washed out of wheat flour dough. It has a chewy, fibrous texture that mimics meat. Because it is made from wheat gluten, it is unsuitable for guests with gluten intolerance or celiac disease.
  79. The correct answer is C (The rice was stirred repeatedly while it simmered under a tight lid.): Stirring pilaf rice while it cooks breaks the grains and releases excess starch, which causes clumping and a gummy texture. Pilaf rice should be left undisturbed once the liquid is added and the pot is covered. Correct technique keeps the grains coated in fat and separate.
  80. The correct answer is D (The rice will fail to release enough starch, producing a noticeably less creamy texture.): Risotto depends on gradual liquid additions combined with constant stirring to slowly release starch from the rice into the surrounding liquid. Dumping all the stock in at once skips this gradual agitation process. The result is a less creamy, less controlled texture than proper risotto technique produces.
  81.   82. Beans must be pre-boiled on the stovetop before being added to a slow cooker preparation.

  88. Seed and grain pairings alone, or doubling up on an already complete protein like quinoa, do not reflect this specific combination principle.

A well-lit shelf near dish pit steam exposes nuts to both heat and moisture, accelerating spoilage. Correct storage protects both flavor quality and shelf life.
